In a world beholden to money – the true Devil's Lettuce – it seems that, lately, it has become skin to a crime to be devoid of funds.

It is not used to prosecute, but, rather, to persecute. > > It's certainly true that, nowadays, homeless people are a dime a dozen.

Over time, society has, unwittingly, created a virtual army of them.

Every town, every city in the world has them, and we've all had our encounters.

Perhaps you saw one at an intersection, holding out his cardboard sign as he approaches your window, or loitering outside of a store you frequent, waiting to ask you for your spare change when you come out.

Nobody has a particularly high opinion of the homeless, and we use all sorts of descriptive terms for them, such as bum, wino, beggar, tramp, bag lady, drifter…and the list goes on. > > As a man who has chosen to live with an open mind, I can completely understand the negative reactions of most people – the looks of disdain, the fleeting gleam of fear in their eyes, the urge toward complete avoidance – and cannot blame them for it.

I am all-too-aware of the fact that I came in far too late in the game to do anything but conform.

The rules had already been established before I even existed, and the myriad droves of arrogant scofflaws who preceded me had already squeezed all the courtesy and civility out of those rules, leaving behind the draconian attitude the homeless are forced to suffer every day. > > since we, unfortunately, have to interact with them personally on a daily basis.

It's not like we enjoy it; it simply, due to my situation, became a necessity of our lifestyle.

The number of instances in which we've made the mistake of befriending someone, only to be swiftly betrayed by that person, is countless.

It took a long time to finally accept that the only way for us to get out of this conundrum was to segregate ourselves completely, as the people around us only ever served to drag us down with them…and the only thing down there was an early grave. > > Out here, you don't have friends.

We are extremely fortunate to have each other. > > My name is Joshua, and I, along with my fiancee, Janina, have been homeless for more than five years now.

We met long before the streets, and have been together for eleven years now.

The kindness quickly drained away from people, and the mean ones felt the shift, and came out to play.

Where, exactly, is a person's income level on that list?

Sadly, shitty people can't resist the urge to take advantage of us because of this.

The fact that we always outwit them in the end does little to diminish the collateral damage they dole out on the way. > > While others sustain themselves with theft, manipulation, and prostitution, I panhandle to make money to support my family.

Other panhandlers are impatient and pushy, only interested in their own instant gratification.

They lie, intimidate people, invade their privacy, and even have the cojones to complain about people being stingy when they only give one dollar. > > I take offense to that. > > No one is obligated to give me anything, and I am entirely grateful for every dollar I receive from those who are willing to take the time to roll down their window and hand it to me.

I don't speak unless I am spoken to, ask for anything other than holding up my sign, and thank everyone who chooses to donate to me profusely, and that gratitude is absolutely genuine. > > Many people may not like what I do, and everyone is entitled to their opinion.

There's certainly no way to know if the person hit me on purpose, so I can't make any accusations.

It's a question that we must address, so that it no longer lingers in everybody's heads. > > Janina and I are certainly not spring chickens.

Though we look much younger than our ages, I am almost forty seven, and she just turned fifty on June seventh.

I was diagnosed, as a child, with a minor case of Scoliosis.

I, myself, have not gone to a doctor for so much as a checkup in at least twenty years, and cannot submit my application without any official documentation of my disabilities, which requires doctor visits which we cannot afford. > > Were traditional avenues available to us, we certainly would already have gotten ourselves out of this situation, but there are a handful of overwhelming obstacles blocking our path. > > The aforementioned lack of help in our area causes us to live, unwittingly, like animals.

Long ago, things weren't nearly as bad.

We used to have money which came regularly and enabled us to get a hotel room at least once per month, but then, those funds were cut off, and now we can only live day to day. > > I'm somewhat ashamed to admit this, but it's the truth: I haven't had a shower in over a year.

I don't have enough clothing to change every day, and anyway, most of the clothing people bring us are women's wear.

Either way, my situation denies me even the most basic level of proper hygiene.

Even brushing our teeth has become a difficulty since all the businesses around us have figured out ways to block us from water. > > OUR FAMILY > > One day, our dog, Cho-Cho, disappeared.

It was very unlike her, and we searched everywhere, frantic, for almost an hour.

When she finally reappeared, she had brought with her a tiny black kitten.

It was pretty much newborn; its eyes hadn't even opened yet. > > What could we do?

Our dog, who had lost her own litter of puppies a couple of years prior, had brought home a new baby.

We even looked, thoroughly, for the rest of the litter, but never found anything, so we had to assume that her mother had died. > > I named the kitten Blackula, and the next day Cho-Cho vanished again for an hour or so.

Though still concerned, we had a good idea, this time, of what she was doing, and, when she returned, we turned out to be right: she'd brought back another kitten, this one a tiger, whom Janina dubbed Pikachu. > > At some point, having been kicked out of every parking lot or field where we'd tried to park the car, we ended up with no choice but to move to a parking spot in the Family Dollar parking lot, a place we'd already been made to leave a year before.

It was next to a major roadway, and the kittens had a tendency to follow us everywhere.

We tried our hardest to be vigilant about locking them in the car whenever we'd leave…but everyone knows it only takes one slip-up.

One night, Janina went to the store while I waited in the car, and Pikachu, unfortunately, followed her.

I'm sure I don't need to explain what happened next.

I've chosen to avoid the details, but suffice it to say that the poor thing didn't have to suffer long.

Thankfully, it was over soon after I laid him down on the ground.

All we could think to do was to tell him how much we loved him as he went. > > THE INCIDENT > > When I first set out to write this GoFundMe, it was prior to the incident I'm about to talk about.

B > > > We ended up being left alone at that spot for close to an entire year, but it was ever in the back of my mind that the proverbial hammer would eventually fall, in which case we had no clue where to go.

A vehicle pulled up, the window rolled down, and it was the owner.

He gave us twenty four hours to move our broken-down car and all of our stuff out of there.

It took an entire day to transport our belongings to the empty field across the street, which was the only place we could even think of to go on such short notice.

The authorities never give us any time to figure out where to go as they do when people are evicted.

Even now, after three days of contemplation, we have not been able to come up with any ideas about where to go from here. > > As an acquaintance of ours named Juan had lived in the same field for a long time, we were already aware that there was a neighbor who lived in one of the houses bordering the field who had a history of getting drunk and causing problems.

He had run Juan's tent over with his pickup truck not knowing if anyone was inside.

When they came, they told us we were fine being there, and advised that we simply ignore Carlos if he tried to antagonize us.

We were perfectly fine when the cops arrived, though, and they left. > > It should have ended there.

Indeed, it certainly seemed like it had.

The authorities had come, twice, and had made it very clear that we, at least for now, had the right to be there.

What more could Carlos really do?

He was an idiot, and we weren't worried about him in the slightest.

We had no problem whatsoever with the suggestion that we ignore him.

It sounded like a fantastic idea. > > Some time later, after midnight, Janina and I, as per usual, were sitting in our car watching a true crime documentary when we began to hear yelling behind us.

I was getting pretty good at tuning him out.

You're **really close**, but what you're seeing is the **default Flask console output**, which tells us: --- ### **You ARE running the server correctly.** That means: - Flask is live. - Your Replit project is listening on port `81`. - Your bot is ready to receive prompts. --- ### **What’s Missing:** Replit hasn’t exposed the **public web URL** for your Flask server yet because we need to do **one small change** to activate their internal web server routing. --- ## **Quick Fix – Use Replit’s Built-In Web Server Port (Port 8080)** Right now, your Flask app is running on **port 81**, but Replit exposes web apps on **port 8080** only. ### Let’s change this: #### 1.

In your `main.py` file, scroll to the bottom and **find this line:** ```python app.run(host="0.0.0.0", port=81) ``` #### 2.

Change it to: ```python app.run(host="0.0.0.0", port=8080) ``` > This tells Replit: “Use the default public web port.” #### 3.

Now, once Flask starts, you should see a message at the top of the Replit console that looks like: ``` Your app is live at: https://runway-video-bot.YOUR_USERNAME.repl.co ``` ### 4.

Tap the small **“open in browser”** icon right next to that URL.

You should see: ``` Runway video bot is active. ``` --- ### Once You See That: 1. **Copy that public URL** (starts with `https://`) 2.

I’ll send your first Arzoragh video prompt directly to your bot and return the Runway video link when it’s ready --- Let me know once you’ve: - Switched the port to `8080` - Restarted the server - Got the **public “live” URL** showing We’re literally on the final step. > **User (2025-03-21 19:41:03 UTC):** > > same problem **Assistant (2025-03-21 19:41:03 UTC):** Thanks for the screenshot—this clears up the issue perfectly. ### The problem: Replit mobile is saying: > **“Port 8080 opened, but not exposed to the web.”** That means the Flask app is running correctly **locally**, but Replit isn’t automatically exposing the public web URL, **which it normally does** on desktop or with `.replit` config. --- ## **Quick Fix: Add a `.replit` Config File** This will tell Replit: > “Run this app like a web server, not a command-line program.” --- ### **Step-by-Step Fix (In the Replit App):** ### 1.

Add a new file - Tap the **“+”** icon to add a file - Name it **`.replit`** (include the dot at the beginning) > This is a config file, not a Python script. --- ### 2.

You and your cousins share grandparents, but your cousins didn’t turn into you.

You both came from the same source, but you branched off in different directions. - Humans and modern apes (like chimpanzees, gorillas, orangutans) all evolved from a now-extinct **common ancestor** that lived around **6 to 8 million years ago**.

That ancestor wasn’t a chimp or gorilla—it was something different, but related to both. - Over time, different groups of that species adapted to different environments and challenges.

One branch eventually became us—**Homo sapiens**—and other branches became chimps, gorillas, and so on.

So, apes are still here because they evolved along their own separate path, just like we did.

Evolution isn’t a ladder—it’s more like a tangled web, with species splitting and adapting in parallel, not in sequence. > **User (2025-03-12 21:30:22 UTC):** > > Are apes less evolved than us then?

And how are we evolved anyway if, when we panic, we get stupid?

Why do we not have any advantage that we evolve to deal with danger?

So let’s tear into it: ### **1.

Are apes less evolved than us?** Nope.

Evolution doesn’t work like that.

It’s not about progress toward perfection—it’s about **adaptation to a specific environment.** - Chimps are extremely well-evolved for their jungle life.

We’re extremely well-evolved for ours.

It only cares about **what works long enough to reproduce**. ### **2.

If panic makes us stupid, how is that evolution?** Perfect question.

But in nature, **fast is often better than smart.** If your ancestors stopped to calculate whether that rustle in the grass was a lion or the wind, they didn’t pass on their genes. ### **3.

So why didn’t we evolve a better danger response?** We did—**for the environments we evolved in.** But evolution is slow, and our environment changed **way faster** than our biology. - Ten thousand years ago, danger meant predators, cliffs, or rival tribes.

Panic made us survive the savannah.

Now it makes us bomb speeches.

That’s not de-evolution—it’s just **lag.** The software update is overdue, but nature doesn’t push patches on demand.

The Human Spine: Built Like a Jenga Tower** - We walk upright, sure—but the spine wasn’t originally designed for that.

Wisdom Teeth: Evolution’s Hoarder Mentality** - Our ancestors needed extra molars for tough, raw diets.

They’re **biological vestigial clutter.** --- ### **8.

Used to fluff fur for warmth or intimidation.

Just awkward. - Tailbone?

It’s what’s left of a tail.

No tail, but hey—we kept the stub. --- ### **So what does this all mean?** Evolution doesn’t build from scratch—it **repurposes**.

Nature isn’t an engineer—it’s a tinkerer.

Our concerns about New Hope extend far beyond simple mismanagement.

A Pattern of False Hope** What follows is a detailed record of how New Hope C.O.R.P.S. subjected us to a pattern of **false hope**, **deliberate delays**, and **eventual betrayal**.

In January 2022, **Janina Miranda** was formally assessed.

Dee-Dee encouraged us to do the assessments separately, claiming it would increase our chances.

Janina even created a list of over **20 available rental listings** and gave them to Dee-Dee to pursue.

Dee-Dee also provided **Suboxone** through New Hope’s outreach to help Joshua detox.

Unfortunately, due to the lack of medical guidance, Joshua went into **precipitated withdrawal** and took the entire supply over a few days, believing it would reset his receptors.

He was honest about what had happened and told Dee-Dee directly.

Instead of addressing the situation supportively, Dee-Dee **never asked Joshua directly about his health**—instead, she violated his privacy by **asking other unhoused individuals if he had relapsed**, and then confronted Janina with the leading statement: **“Be honest.”** After that, she **ghosted us for several months**, ignoring **over 100 texts**.

Then, one day, she **reappeared without warning**, clearly disinterested in our situation.

She said New Hope’s budget had run out, and they’d get new funding in **May**.

But when May came and went, and we reached out to her, she claimed she had told us **September** instead.

That was the **last time we ever heard from Dee-Dee**.

Her abandonment, privacy violations, and vague shifting promises destroyed months of momentum.

Her departure, however, did not mean her influence disappeared.

He told us he had reviewed our file and seemed remorseful—not direct in his apology, but clearly regretful.

He asked us to give him the chance to “**fix what Dee-Dee did**.” We were stunned.

After everything, the idea that someone within the same agency was admitting fault gave us cautious hope.

Naturally, we allowed him to try.

After all, **all we’ve ever wanted was a home**. **Adam only came the one time.** We didn’t hear from him again for **seven months**—but we didn’t push for updates during that time.

When we finally reached out in **July 2023**, Adam **acted like he didn’t know who we were**.

When reminded, he apologized and promised to meet with us the next day at 11 a.m.

He apologized again and rescheduled for the following day. **He ghosted us again—and that was the last we ever heard from Adam.** It is still unclear what his purpose was, or why he reached out in the first place.

That brief glimmer of honesty—his recognition that we’d been wronged—was snuffed out the moment he vanished. **To this day, we are bewildered as to what his endgame was.** It felt like someone staged an apology only to **remind us who holds the power**. --- #### **False Hope Instance #3 – Stephen Heath’s Slippery Support** **(August 2023 – January 2024)** Following Adam’s disappearance, we were assigned to **Stephen Heath**.

At first, Stephen projected professionalism.

He collected our documents, reviewed our file, and told us we would need to **wait for the release of HUD vouchers**.

He never gave a clear date—even when we told him we’d heard they were expected to be released on **October 1st**, which was publicly listed on HUD’s site.

When we asked directly, Stephen wouldn’t confirm it.

He remained vague, his cynical tone growing more noticeable with each visit.

Still, we wanted to believe in him.

At one point, fully convinced he was diligently working on our case, Joshua texted Stephen a message **thanking him for his efforts** and letting him know his work mattered.

Stephen never replied to the text.

But the next time he saw us, he said something we’ll never forget: > “Don’t thank me yet.” At the time, we thought it was humility.

In hindsight, it was ominous.

Stephen would later contradict himself multiple times—about timelines, about program rules, and most critically, about **income requirements**.

At one point, he told us outright that **having no income was a benefit** in the program.

But later, under pressure, he’d claim the only reason we weren’t housed was because we didn’t submit proof of income.

This revelation was deeply unsettling, so we texted Stephen for clarity.

His response was curt: he said it was now **“out of his hands”** and gave us a phone number for someone named **Taylor**.

We assumed Taylor was our new contact with HUD, and for the first time in months, we felt like progress was imminent.

But when we called Taylor, the experience was devastating.

She **had no idea who we were**, claimed to have never heard of our case, and—most shocking of all—**denied knowing what Rapid Rehousing even was.** This was the final red flag.

Her **LinkedIn profile clearly lists her as the Senior Director of Homeless Services at New Hope**, overseeing Rapid Rehousing, Stability Vouchers, and program outcomes.

When we pointed this out to her, she became angry and dismissive, accusing Janina of **“throwing a tantrum”** for not getting her way.

The call ended abruptly.

And within an hour of posting about the contradiction on **New Hope’s Facebook page**, our comment was deleted.

We went right back to Stephen.

It had “returned to his hands,” whether he liked it or not.

But Stephen didn’t handle it well.

Between that phone call and the illegal eviction in January, Stephen showed up **at least twice**.

Except—**he had never given us a number for Homeless Trust.** Nor had he ever instructed us to call them.

He was doing his best to convince us that we had simply forgotten, but we hadn’t.

His behavior was visibly uncomfortable and increasingly awkward.

We didn’t challenge him.

It was obvious that **Stephen was trying to extract himself from the situation**.

He had tried to do so with Taylor—but when that failed, it fell back on him.

We called Homeless Trust right after he left, only to be told by the woman who answered: > “The Homeless Trust doesn’t deal with the public.” We also contacted **HUD**.

One number led us to a man who claimed not to know what Rapid Rehousing was.

Still, we gave New Hope the benefit of the doubt.

We told ourselves maybe they hadn’t checked the right list.

We desperately wanted to believe Stephen had been trying his best, that there was just a miscommunication.

But even **confirmation bias** has limits.

New Hope had been stringing us along for far too long.

The Illegal Eviction** In January 2024, events reached a breaking point.

He pretended to be offering help and made a phone call to **Taylor Alvarez** right in front of us.

He said New Hope would send a team to assist us.

That team never came.

Days later, **Stephen Heath** showed up instead.

He began by lamenting that posting “those 5-day notices” made him look bad with the homeless community.

Then, without warning, he pulled out one of those notices and **posted it himself**, affixing it to a nearby pole.

We pointed this out repeatedly.

He ignored everything and left.

It was a full operation.

Our belongings were **trashed, stolen, or destroyed on-site.** Some items—like a locked chest—were taken by the man hired by the bank to remove our property.

Pets were lost or killed.

Stephen showed up during the eviction and participated without hesitation.

He wasn’t an outreach worker.

To this day, we don’t know what his title actually is.

Some items had been painstakingly replaced after previous sweeps.

On multiple occasions, we found **our possessions deliberately smashed before being thrown into dumpsters**, just to prevent us from salvaging anything.

This wasn’t “cleaning up” an encampment.

It was the **passive elimination** of human beings—done not with bullets, but with erasure.

New Hope didn’t just fail to protect us; they helped engineer our removal, while pretending to be our advocates.

And even after all this, we tried to maintain communication.

In the aftermath, Joshua sent Stephen a **diplomatic, disappointed text message**, not out of anger, but out of a desperate need for clarity.

He **showed up in person, shaking with rage**, and accused Joshua of being disrespectful.

This was the same person who once received a heartfelt message of gratitude from us.

Structural Rot – The Homeless Trust and HUD OIG** Our experiences with New Hope did not occur in a vacuum.

This means the **organization responsible for overseeing New Hope is led, in part, by New Hope's own leadership**.

Even so, we still called the Homeless Trust.

It almost felt like **some higher force shoved us in the right direction** just as we were about to step into a particularly ravenous patch of quicksand.

Perhaps a situation like this cannot be allowed to stand.

There was no structured communication, no case management, and no transparency. 2. **Contradictory messaging about income:** Initially, Stephen told us that **having no income was ideal** for RRH.

Later, under pressure and in front of MDPD officers, he claimed our **lack of income** was the reason we weren’t housed.

Stephen tried to offload us to Taylor.

Taylor denied knowing what RRH even was.

Every failed interaction further proved we were not being helped—we were being worn down. #### **HUD Expectations vs.

Violations of CARF Accreditation Standards** As a CARF-accredited organization, **New Hope C.O.R.P.S.** is expected to uphold strict standards regarding **ethical leadership**, **client dignity**, **confidentiality**, and **accountability**.

The conduct we've documented here reflects systematic and repeated violations of those very principles.

Leadership and Ethical Practices** - **Stephen Alvarez** holds dual roles as both the Executive Director of New Hope and a **Provider Representative** on the board of the Miami-Dade Homeless Trust, which funds his own organization.

Taylor’s LinkedIn profile clearly states that she manages **RRH and voucher programs**, yet she **claimed not to know what Rapid Rehousing was** during a phone call with us. - When confronted with this contradiction, Taylor became angry and retaliatory—accusing us of "having a tantrum" for requesting accountability.

A public comment we made highlighting the discrepancy was deleted from New Hope’s Facebook page within the hour. --- #### **2.

Confidentiality and Privacy (HIPAA-Adjacent Violations)** - Dee-Dee, instead of asking Joshua about his health, asked **another homeless person** if he had relapsed, and then pressured Janina with the phrase: “Be honest.” - Taylor became defensive when Janina mentioned this and showed no interest in investigating the breach.

The Personal and Emotional Toll** We have been **unhoused since 2018**—not due to drugs or criminal behavior, but from a tragic series of losses that snowballed into systemic entrapment.

For years, we did everything right: - We submitted all necessary paperwork. - We completed assessments and followed all instructions. - We exercised patience and restraint, even when severely mistreated.

Instead, we lost everything we owned—**multiple times**.

Our **pets were killed**, **our property destroyed**, and our safety jeopardized.

In one particularly cruel pattern, our things were not just thrown away—they were intentionally **broken first**, so we couldn’t retrieve them.

Janina’s **sister’s ashes** were among the irreplaceable items lost in one of these sweeps.

Even after the illegal eviction, we held our composure.

We tithed when we had nothing.

We trusted that good behavior would eventually lead to good outcomes.

This is not a matter of comfort.

This is a matter of **basic safety**.

We are unhoused through no fault of our own.

We were repeatedly told we were on priority lists, told to wait patiently, told we were being helped—and instead, we were sabotaged, displaced, and abandoned.

Why We Call Them “No Hope”** What kind of organization uses its trusted relationship with vulnerable people to trick them into displacement?

What kind of nonprofit punishes honesty and rewards silence?

An organization that was once called **New Hope** became, to us and many others on the street, something far more dangerous.

We now believe that we were, from the start, labeled **NHI** by the very agencies tasked with helping us.

What followed was **passive elimination**.

Not murder, not exile—just **slow erasure** through red tape, exhaustion, false hope, and the withholding of critical support.

You’re never told directly that you don’t matter.
